<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> We prove that a smooth Ricci flow on a closed real four-manifold extends on the same manifold through every finite time at which its scalar curvature remains uniformly bounded. This resolves the scalar-curvature extension problem in dimension four.

- **claim-smooth-extension** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-extension)): Let $M$ be a closed connected smooth four-manifold, let $0<T<\infty$, and let $g(t)$, $0\le t<T$, be a smooth Ricci flow with smooth initial metric. If $$\sup_{M\times[0,T)}\|R(g(t))\|<\infty,$$ then there are $\varepsilon>0$ and a smooth Ricci flow $\widetilde g(t)$ on $M$, $0\le t<T+\varepsilon$, such that $\widetilde g(t)=g(t)$ for every $t<T$.
  - Explanation: A smooth Ricci flow on a closed four-manifold with uniformly bounded scalar curvature on a finite interval continues smoothly past the final time on the same manifold, agreeing with the original flow before that time.
- **claim-scalar-blowup** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cor-scalar-blowup)): If a Ricci flow on a closed smooth four-manifold has finite maximal smooth existence time $T$, then $$\limsup_{t\uparrow T}\max_{x\in M}R(g(t))(x)=+\infty.$$
  - Explanation: Finite maximal smooth existence time forces the maximum of the scalar curvature to be unbounded as the time approaches the singular time; this is the contrapositive form of the extension theorem.
- **claim-functional-first-variation** (theorem, [source](#stmt-ell-functional)): The functional in Equation <a href="#eq:energy-functional" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:energy-functional">[eq:energy-functional]</a> is defined on the preceding metrics. For a variation with the same root decay, $$DE_g(\dot g)=\int\left\langle\tfrac12R(g)g-\mathop{\mathrm{Ric}}(g),\dot g\right\rangle_g \,d\mu_g. \label{ell:first-variation}$$ It is invariant under diffeomorphisms isotopic to the identity whose root displacement, including scaled derivatives throughout the isotopy, is $O(r^{1-q'})$ for some $q'>1$. A global length scaling by $s$, accompanied by a coordinate dilation at infinity restoring the leading tensor $\delta$, multiplies $E$ by $s^2$. Under Equation <a href="#eq:tree-ricci-error" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:tree-ricci-error">[eq:tree-ricci-error]</a>, $\|E(g)\|\le CM$.
  - Explanation: The renormalized functional is well defined on the tree metrics, has a first variation pairing the metric velocity with the Einstein tensor, is invariant under suitably decaying isotopic diffeomorphisms, scales quadratically, and is bounded by the weighted Ricci error.
- **claim-static-tree-inequality** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-thm-tree-inequality)): For the fixed tree and the sequences just specified, $$E(g)=o(M). \label{ell:little-oh}$$ For $M=0$ the assertion means $E(g)=0$.
  - Explanation: Imported from the companion article: on a fixed limiting tree the renormalized functional is small compared with the weighted Ricci error along the specified sequences.
- **claim-scale-degeneration** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-degeneration)): For a sufficiently small cap and all sufficiently late times, the following conclusions hold.* **Scale and energy bounds.* One has $$\begin{aligned} cr^4\leq\mathop{\mathrm{Vol}}_t B_t(x,r)&\leq Cr^4, &\int_M\|\mathop{\mathrm{Rm}}\|^2\,d\mu_t&\leq C,\label{geom:volume-energy}\\ \|\nabla^k\mathop{\mathrm{Rm}}\|(x,t)&\leq C_k l(x,t)^{-2-k}, &\|\nabla^k\mathop{\mathrm{Ric}}\|(x,t)&\leq C_k l(x,t)^{-1-k}. \label{eq:ricci-improvement} \end{aligned}$$ Here $0<r\leq l_0$ and each fixed $k$ is allowed. The set $\{l<r\}$ is covered by a uniformly bounded number of balls of radius $Cr$. Distances converge uniformly to a compact metric quotient $Z$ of the material manifold. Away from finitely many concentration points the metric convergence is smooth.* **Small-scale limits and genuine singularities.* At arbitrary sequences of centers and length scales $h_i\to0$, with times tending to $T$, a subsequence of the rescaled slices converges to a complete noncollapsed Ricci-flat orbifold, smoothly away from finitely many genuine orbifold points. Each such point has a nontrivial finite group acting freely on $S^3$. A nonflat limit has a single Euclidean quotient end with nontrivial group. A flat limit is a global Euclidean quotient and has at most one exceptional point.* **Finite trees.* Within each concentrating cluster, maximal inequivalent nonflat limits form a finite rooted tree with a unique outermost entry. The joining regions have the quantitative charts of Lemma <a href="#geom:neck" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="geom:neck">7</a> below.
  - Explanation: At late times the flow has two-sided volume bounds, bounded curvature energy, derivative bounds in terms of the curvature radius, smooth convergence away from finitely many points, orbifold small-scale limits and a finite rooted tree of nonflat limits.
- **claim-neck-improvement** (theorem, [source](#stmt-geom-neck)): Suppose annular regions have inner and outer radii $r_-\ll r_+$ and every escaping intermediate annulus converges smoothly to a round flat quotient annulus. Assume for some $\sigma>0$ and all needed finite orders that $$r^2\|\mathop{\mathrm{Ric}}\|_{k+2,r}\leq C\big((r/r_+)^\sigma+(r_-/r)^\sigma\big).$$ Fix $0<\mu<\min(1,\sigma)$. After removing fixed-factor boundary collars, for this fixed exponent, $$\label{eq:neck-decay} r^2\|\mathop{\mathrm{Rm}}\|_{k,r}\leq C\big((r/r_+)^\mu+(r_-/r)^\mu\big).$$ A single equivariant coordinate system on a shortened neck gives the same bound for $g-\delta$, with scaled derivatives. Infinite half-necks are allowed. One may also use slowly varying coefficients on the boundary terms, when the forcing and boundary data obey their corresponding bounds.
  - Explanation: On necks that converge to round flat quotient annuli, a weighted Ricci bound with some exponent improves to a weighted curvature bound and to equivariant coordinates in which the metric is close to Euclidean.
- **claim-continuous-root-scale** (construction, [source](#stmt-prop-root-scale)): There is a fixed small $c_*>0$ such that the following definition detects the unique outermost entry. Choose a bounded continuous $\psi:[0,1]\to[0,\infty)$, zero on $[0,c_*]$ and strictly positive on $[2c_*,1]$, and set $$\label{geom:root-definition} a(t)=\sup_{y\in\mathcal U} l(y,t)\psi\big(l(y,t)^2\|\mathop{\mathrm{Rm}}\|(y,t)\big).$$ After shrinking $\mathcal U$, this supremum is attained in its interior for every late time; it is positive and continuous, and $a(t)\to0$. For every sequence of late times, scale $a(t)$ and any maximizing point represent the outermost nonflat entry. Every other nonflat entry has scale bounded by a constant times $a(t)$. The constants may be fixed after extracting the sequential tree.
  - Explanation: A cutoff of the scale-invariant curvature, weighted by the curvature radius and maximized over a neighbourhood, gives a positive continuous function tending to zero that detects the outermost nonflat entry of the tree.
- **claim-geodesic-radius** (theorem, [source](#stmt-geom-geodesic-radius)): For any minimizing segment $\gamma:[0,D]\to M$ at a late time, parametrized by arclength, $$l(\gamma(s),t)\geq c\min(l_0,s,D-s),\qquad 0<s<D.$$ Consequently, whenever $d_t(x,y)\leq Ch$ with $h$ small, its upper and lower time derivatives satisfy $$\label{geom:distance-log} \|\partial_t d_t(x,y)\|\leq C'\left(1+\log^+(h/l(x,t))+\log^+(h/l(y,t))\right).$$ Time derivatives can be interpreted almost everywhere or by barriers.
  - Explanation: Along a minimizing segment the curvature radius is bounded below by the distance to the endpoints, which yields a logarithmic bound on the time derivative of distances.
- **claim-slow-motion** (theorem, [source](#stmt-geom-slow-motion)): Let $h_i\to0$ and let $J_i$ be time intervals of length $\delta_i=o(h_i)$ tending to $T$. Distances initially of order $O(h_i)$ change by $o(h_i)$ throughout $J_i$. If a material point has $l\geq c h_i$ at one time, it has $l\geq c' h_i$ throughout (after extraction for a sequential assertion). Regular compact charts at this scale persist, and their scaled metric coefficients, with any fixed finite number of scaled derivatives, change by $O(\delta_i/h_i)$ in fixed material coordinates.
  - Explanation: On time intervals short compared with the length scale, distances, the curvature radius at regular points and regular chart coefficients move only by a small amount relative to that scale.
- **claim-scale-change-time** (theorem, [source](#stmt-geom-scale-change-time)): Fix $u>0$. Let $s_i<t_i<T$ tend to $T$, where $t_i$ is the first time after $s_i$ for which $\|\log(a(t_i)/a(s_i))\|=u$. Then $$\frac{t_i-s_i}{a(s_i)^2}\longrightarrow\infty.$$
  - Explanation: A fixed factor change of the outermost scale takes a duration that is large compared with the squared scale, so the root geometry is frozen on shorter durations.
- **claim-buffered-intervals** (construction, [source](#stmt-prop-buffered-intervals)): If a concentration point exists, there are parabolically rescaled intervals $I=[-\theta,1+\theta]$, for one fixed $\theta>0$, with physical squared length units $d_i\to0$, such that the root scale throughout $I$ is comparable to a number $a_i\to0$ and changes by a fixed factor between $0$ and $1$. Put $\epsilon_i=\sqrt{d_i}$. In these units $$\|R\|\leq C\epsilon_i^2,\qquad \|\mathop{\mathrm{Ric}}\|_{k,r}\leq C_k\epsilon_i/r$$ at regularity scales $r$ in a bounded range. For $a_i\ll r\lesssim1$ the cluster has flat quotient annular limits with nontrivial group. At any fixed time, allowable root centers differ by $O(a_i)$.
  - Explanation: If a concentration point exists, one can select parabolically rescaled buffered intervals on which the root scale is comparable to a vanishing number and changes by a fixed factor between the endpoints, with the stated curvature bounds.
- **claim-rescaled-motion** (theorem, [source](#stmt-geom-rescaled-motion)): On the intervals of Proposition <a href="#prop:buffered-intervals" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:buffered-intervals">12</a>, geometry at length $h\lesssim1$ moves negligibly over windows $\Delta t$ satisfying $\epsilon_i\Delta t/h\to0$. Regular chart coefficients change by $O(\epsilon_i\Delta t/h)$ with every fixed number of scaled derivatives. At $h\asymp a_i$, old and new centers are $O(a_i)$ apart; at $a_i\ll h\lesssim1$ they are $o(h)$ apart.
  - Explanation: In interval units, geometry moves negligibly on short windows, regular charts change by a controlled amount, and the root centers stay within the root scale or are negligible relative to larger scales.
- **claim-exterior-geometry** (theorem, [source](#stmt-geom-exterior-geometry)): Fix a sufficiently high finite derivative order $k_{\mathrm{geo}}$ and numbers $0<\mu<\sigma<1$. There are fixed constants $N_{\mathrm{geom}},C,c_{\mathrm{geom}}>0$ and outer radii $R_i^{\mathrm{out}}\to\infty$ in interval units, with $\epsilon_iR_i^{\mathrm{out}}\to0$, with the following property. At every time in the buffered interval the exterior region admits one equivariant coordinate system whose annuli satisfy $$\label{geom:uniform-exterior} r^2\|\mathop{\mathrm{Rm}}\|_{k_{\mathrm{geo}},r}+\|g-\delta\|_{k_{\mathrm{geo}},r} \le C\Theta_i(r),\qquad \Theta_i(r)=(a_i/r)^\mu+\delta_i r^\mu,\qquad \delta_i=(R_i^{\mathrm{out}})^{-\mu}\longrightarrow0$$ for $N_{\mathrm{geom}}a_i\le r\le c_{\mathrm{geom}}R_i^{\mathrm{out}}$. The charts cover whole shortened distance bands with boundary margins; their nontrivial quotient group is fixed after subsequence extraction. Moreover $\epsilon_i=o(\delta_i)$. The constant $C$ is uniform in time and radius and is unchanged when the lower bound is restricted to $Na_i$ for any larger fixed $N$.
  - Explanation: Outside a fixed multiple of the root scale the exterior carries equivariant coordinates with curvature and metric error controlled by a two-term weight, uniformly in time.
- **claim-local-smoothing** (theorem, [source](#stmt-para-local-smoothing)): Consider a linear system on a regular parabolic patch, normalized to spatial radius and temporal duration of order one, with scalar uniformly elliptic principal part. Suppose its spatial coefficient derivatives through a sufficiently high fixed order are bounded. For nested cylinders $Q'\Subset Q$ whose evaluation times have a positive past margin, a solution $U$ with source $F$ satisfies, for $1<p\le2$, $$\sup_{Q'}\sum_{k=0}^j\|D^kU\| \le C\left[ \left(\frac1{\|J\|}\int_J\\|U(\cdot,\tau)\\|_{L^2(\Omega)}^p\,d\tau\right)^{1/p} +\left(\frac1{\|J\|}\int_J\\|F(\cdot,\tau)\\|_{H^{j+3}(\Omega)}^p\,d\tau\right)^{1/p} \right], \label{para:local-mixed}$$ after enlarging $\Omega\times J$ inside $Q$ if necessary. The first input can instead be the normalized spacetime $L^p$ norm of $U$. Constants depend on the coefficient bounds and margins. Corresponding time $L^p$ estimates over longer windows follow by integrating sliding cylinders of unit duration.
  - Explanation: Interior parabolic smoothing: spatial derivatives of a solution on a smaller cylinder are bounded by mixed time-integrability norms of the solution and the source on a larger cylinder.
- **claim-radial-inequality** (theorem, [source](#stmt-para-radial-lemma)): Suppose $PZ=Z$, $Z$ is compactly supported in time, and $E=(\partial_\tau-\Delta_\delta)Z$. For $1<p\le2$, set $$z(s)=\left\\|\,\\|Z(s,\cdot,\tau)\\|_{L^2(S^3)}\,\right\\|_{L^p_\tau}.$$ With either ordinary or constant-normalized time measure, $$\mathcal Lz:=-z''-\frac3s z'+\frac3{s^2}z \le\left\\|\,\\|E(s,\cdot,\tau)\\|_{L^2(S^3)}\,\right\\|_{L^p_\tau}. \label{para:radial-inequality}$$ For $p=2$ the same conclusion holds for a Hilbert direct sum of fields in different charts or time windows with the same radial variable. The homogeneous comparison powers are $s$ and $s^{-3}$.
  - Explanation: For a field without spherical mean, a radial differential inequality holds for its sphere-and-time norm, using the spectral gap of the three-sphere and comparison powers one and minus three.
- **claim-mean-derivatives** (theorem, [source](#stmt-para-mean-lemma)): Let $T$ be a spatially constant multiple of $\mathop{\mathrm{Ric}}$ on an annulus whose metric has scaled $C^{j+1}$ distance at most $e$ from $\delta$. On a fixed-width shell of radius $s$ in its Cartesian coordinates, $$\sum_{k=1}^j s^k\|\nabla^kT\| \le C_j\left(\sum_{k=1}^j s^k\sup\|D^kPT\| +e\sup\|T\|_{j,s}\right), \label{para:mean-derivatives}$$ where the suprema on the right use a fixed enlargement of the shell.
  - Explanation: Positive-order derivatives of the spherical mean of a Ricci multiple are bounded by derivatives of the oscillation part plus metric-error terms, thanks to the divergence constraint.
- **claim-exterior-ricci** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-exterior-ricci)): There are fixed $N_0\gg1$, $r_0>0$, and $0<\alpha<\min(\mu,1)$, and a choice of $N_1$ in Equation <a href="#eq:root-energy" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:root-energy">[eq:root-energy]</a>, such that $$\begin{aligned} V(r)&\le C\left[(a/r)^{2+\alpha}K +\widetilde\delta_i\bigl((a/r)^\alpha+r^\alpha\bigr)\right], \label{para:exterior-v}\\ A(r)&\le C\left[(a/r)^{2+\alpha}K+\widetilde\delta_i\right] \label{para:exterior-a} \end{aligned}$$ for $N_0a\le r\le r_0$, where $\widetilde\delta_i\to0$ and $\widetilde\delta_i\ge\epsilon$. The constants are independent of $i$ and $r$.
  - Explanation: On dyadic exterior annuli the cut-off Ricci tensor and its derivatives decay faster than the dimensional rate, with a leading term proportional to the root energy and vanishing error terms.
- **claim-maximal-function-bound** (bound, [source](#stmt-para-maximal-lemma)): For every fixed $D_1$, $$\\|H_*\\|_{L^2(\mathbb{R})}\le C_{D_1,p}(K+o_i(1)). \label{eq:maximal-ricci}$$
  - Explanation: The maximal function built from the root energy density is bounded in square-integrable norm by the root energy up to a vanishing error.
- **claim-interior-ricci-weighted** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-interior-ricci)): Take any sequence of times with $\eta(t)\ne0$ and extract a tree as in Theorem <a href="#thm:degeneration" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:degeneration">6</a>. On its true compact and half-neck pieces in $B_t(x_t,D_0a)$, let $r$ be the physical length of the local regular patch, in $I$-units. For every fixed required derivative order $j$ there are $C,\nu>0$ such that $$(r/a)^2\|G\|_{j,r}(t) \le C\bigl(H_*(t)+\epsilon\bigr)e^{-\nu D}. \label{eq:interior-weighted}$$ Here $D=0$ on compact pieces, including truncated outer compact regions. On a joining neck $D$, up to bounded shifts, is the minimum of the logarithmic distances from the two ends. It is the half-neck coordinate used in Section <a href="#sec:static-input" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:static-input">2</a>. Constants may depend on the extracted tree, and the exponent may be decreased.
  - Explanation: At a single time, on the compact and neck pieces of an extracted tree, the weighted Ricci size is bounded by the maximal function plus a small term with exponential decay in the neck depth.
- **claim-collar-extension** (construction, [source](#stmt-thm-collar-extension)): There are $\sigma_*>0$, $R_*<\infty$, and $C<\infty$, depending only on $q,k$, the fixed shell choices, and the weight comparison constants, with the following property. If $\sigma\le\sigma_*$ and $R\ge R_*$, there is a metric $\bar h$ on $\mathcal E$ such that $$\begin{split} \\|\bar h-\delta\\|_{H^k_q} &\le C\\|g-\delta\\|_{H^k(C_1)},\\ \\|g-\bar h\\|_{H^k(C_1)} +\\|\mathop{\mathrm{Ric}}(\bar h)\\|_{H^{k-2}_{q+2}} &\le C m_1. \end{split} \label{eq:collar-extension}$$ The map $g\|_{C_1}\mapsto\bar h-\delta$ is real analytic on a fixed neighborhood of zero in collar $H^k$, and its first derivative into $H^k_q$ has uniformly bounded operator norm. It is independent of $R$, the weights, and the choice of an exterior realizing the collar data. There is also such an analytic extension $\hat h$, equal to $g$ near the inner boundary, for which $$\\|\hat h-\delta\\|_{H^k_q}\le C\\|g-\delta\\|_{H^k(C_1)}, \qquad \\|\mathop{\mathrm{Ric}}(\hat h)\\|_{H^{k-2}_{q+2}}\le Cm_1,$$ and whose first derivative has the same bound.
  - Explanation: A real-analytic extension of a fixed collar metric to a Euclidean-quotient exterior exists with weighted norm control and Ricci error controlled by the collar residual, independently of the exterior length.
- **claim-local-potential-korn** (theorem, [source](#stmt-collar-potential)): On a fixed smooth Euclidean annulus, ball, or fixed-width annular overlap in dimension four, there is a bounded linear extraction $v\mapsto P v\in H^{k+1}$ with $$\\|v-\mathcal L_{Pv}\delta\\|_{H^k} \le C\\|\mathcal K(v)\\|_{H^{k-2}}, \qquad \\|Pv\\|_{H^{k+1}}\le C\\|v\\|_{H^k}. \label{collar:local-potential}$$ For vector fields on these domains, $$\inf_{W\in\mathfrak e(4)}\\|V-W\\|_{H^{k+1}} \le C\\|\mathcal L_V\delta\\|_{H^k}, \label{collar:korn}$$ where $\mathfrak e(4)=\{x\mapsto Ax+b:A^t=-A\}$. All choices can be linear, normalized modulo this rigid-motion space, and equivariant. After dilation, the right side of the first inequality in Equation <a href="#collar:local-potential" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="collar:local-potential">[collar:local-potential]</a> is multiplied by $r^2$, and vector norms in both inequalities are divided by $r$.
  - Explanation: On fixed Euclidean domains, a symmetric tensor is a Lie derivative of a potential up to an error controlled by linearized curvature, and vector fields satisfy a Korn estimate modulo rigid motions.
- **claim-newton-estimates** (bound, [source](#stmt-collar-newton)): Let $G(x)=c_4\|x\|^{-2}$ be the fundamental solution for the chosen sign of the Euclidean Laplacian. For $0<q<2$, its Newton operator is bounded $$\Delta^{-1}:H^{k-2}_{q+2}(\mathbb{R}^4)\longrightarrow H^k_q(\mathbb{R}^4).$$ For $1<q<2$, if $h\in H^{k-3}_{q+3}(\mathbb{R}^4)$ and $\int_{\mathbb{R}^4}h=0$ componentwise, then $$\\|\Delta^{-1}h\\|_{H^{k-1}_{q+1}} \le C\\|h\\|_{H^{k-3}_{q+3}}. \label{collar:zero-mass-newton}$$ Finally, on an annulus or ball of outer radius comparable to $R$, if $\Delta W=H$, boundary strips control $W$ in scaled $H^{k-2}$ by $b_0$, and $c_r=r^2\\|H\\|_{H^{k-4}(r)}$, then $$\sup_r\\|W\\|_{H^{k-2}(r)}\le C\left(b_0+\sum_rc_r\right). \label{collar:green-sum}$$ The constant is independent of the number of shells. A filled ball has no inner boundary term; its innermost shell is replaced by a fixed ball.
  - Explanation: The Newton operator is bounded between the weighted Sobolev spaces, gains a decay power for zero-mass sources, and a Green-function sum bound is independent of the number of shells.
- **claim-collar-position** (theorem, [source](#stmt-final-collar-position)): Choose sufficiently small $\sigma_0>0$ and sufficiently large fixed $N$. At a time near $\mathop{\mathrm{supp}}\eta$, choose an exterior annular chart at scale $b$ with domain, in collar units, $\{1/8<\|x\|<16\}/\Gamma$, retaining slightly larger boundary margins when necessary. Keep its map into the material manifold and its group fixed. For all sufficiently late $i$, throughout any interval on which its metric stays within $\sigma_0$ of the flat metric through the required fixed derivative order, the following assertions hold. Every closed shortened collar lies at distance comparable to $b$ from the current center $x_t$; its middle bands cover the corresponding shortened distance annuli. The component on the designated inner side of a middle sphere contains $B_t(x_t,N_1a)$ and is contained in $B_t(x_t,Cb)$, with fixed constants.
  - Explanation: A controlled material collar chart stays at distance comparable to its radius from the root center, covers the corresponding distance annuli, and its inner side contains the root ball and sits inside a comparable ball.
- **claim-collar-time-bound** (bound, [source](#stmt-final-collar-bound)): The residual in Equation <a href="#eq:collar-residual" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:collar-residual">[eq:collar-residual]</a> for every active patch is bounded by a common nonnegative function $m(t)$ such that $$\label{eq:collar-time-bound} \sup_{\mathop{\mathrm{supp}}\eta}m=o_i(1),\qquad b^{-2}\\|\eta m\\|_{L^2(I)}+\\|\eta B_1\\|_{L^2(I)} \le C\bigl[(a/b)^{2+\alpha}K+\rho_i\bigr], \quad \rho_i\longrightarrow0.$$ The leading constant is independent of large $N$; $\rho_i$ may depend on fixed $N$.
  - Explanation: The collar residual is bounded by a common function that is small uniformly, and its time-integrated size is controlled by the root energy times a scale ratio power plus a vanishing term.
- **claim-variation-lower-bound** (bound, [source](#stmt-final-variation)): For the preceding extensions and partition, $$\label{eq:variation-lower} \sum_j\int_I\eta^2\chi_j E_j'\,dt \ge 2a^4K^2-a^4\bigl[CN^{-2\alpha}K^2 +o_i(1)(K+1)^2\bigr].$$
  - Explanation: The weighted sum of functional derivatives over the partition is bounded below by a positive multiple of the squared root energy minus collar tail loss and vanishing errors.
- **claim-functional-smallness** (bound, [source](#stmt-final-functional-smallness)): For fixed $N$ there are numbers $\zeta_i\to0$ such that, at every time in every active patch, $$\label{eq:functional-littleoh} \|\eta(t)E_j(t)\|\le \zeta_i a^4\bigl[H_*(t)+1+a^{-2}\eta(t)m(t)\bigr].$$ Here $H_*$ uses a fixed enlargement depending on $N$.
  - Explanation: Applying the static inequality to extracted trees shows that the functional on every active patch is small relative to the maximal function, one and the scaled collar residual.
- **claim-energy-vanishes** (theorem, [source](#stmt-final-energy-vanishes)): Along the buffered intervals, $K_i\to0$.
  - Explanation: Combining the lower variation bound with the upper smallness bound after fixing the collar factor forces the normalized root Ricci energy to tend to zero, even without prior boundedness.
- **claim-ricci-envelope** (bound, [source](#stmt-final-ricci-envelope)): On a slightly smaller material neighborhood of the isolated cluster, for $0\le t\le1$, $$\label{eq:final-ricci-bound} \|\mathop{\mathrm{Ric}}\|(y,t)\le Q_*(t)\bigl[1+(a/l(y,t))^2\bigr], \qquad\\|Q_*\\|_{L^2([0,1])}\longrightarrow0.$$
  - Explanation: Near the cluster the Ricci tensor is bounded by a time function with vanishing square-integral norm times a factor involving the ratio of root scale to curvature radius.
- **claim-good-points** (theorem, [source](#stmt-final-good-points)): There is a single set of material points $\mathcal G_i$, independent of time, whose complement has volume $o(a_i^4)$, which is $o(a_i)$-dense at every $t\in[0,1]$ in a smaller neighborhood of the cluster. For points $x,y\in\mathcal G_i$ in that neighborhood whose distance at either endpoint is at most $Ca_i$, $$\label{final:good-distance} \|d_1(x,y)-d_0(x,y)\|=o(a_i),$$ uniformly for each fixed $C$.
  - Explanation: A time-independent set of material points with small complement is dense at the root scale at every time, and distances between its points at root scale change by a negligible amount.
- **claim-root-maximum-fixed** (theorem, [source](#stmt-final-root-fixed)): Let $\mathfrak a_i(t)$ denote the root scale of Proposition <a href="#prop:root-scale" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:root-scale">8</a>, computed in interval units. Then $$a_i^{-1}\bigl\|\mathfrak a_i(1)-\mathfrak a_i(0)\bigr\| \longrightarrow0.$$
  - Explanation: The root scale at the two endpoints of the buffered interval differs by a negligible amount relative to the scale, contradicting the prescribed fixed factor change and ruling out concentration.

        "anchor": "stmt-thm-degeneration",
        "exact_quote": "For a sufficiently small cap and all sufficiently late times, the following conclusions hold.*\n\n\n\n**Scale and energy bounds.* One has $$\\begin{aligned}\n cr^4\\leq\\mathop{\\mathrm{Vol}}_t B_t(x,r)&\\leq Cr^4,\n       &\\int_M|\\mathop{\\mathrm{Rm}}|^2\\,d\\mu_t&\\leq C,\\label{geom:volume-energy}\\\\\n |\\nabla^k\\mathop{\\mathrm{Rm}}|(x,t)&\\leq C_k l(x,t)^{-2-k},\n       &|\\nabla^k\\mathop{\\mathrm{Ric}}|(x,t)&\\leq C_k l(x,t)^{-1-k}.\n       \\label{eq:ricci-improvement}\n\\end{aligned}$$ Here $0<r\\leq l_0$ and each fixed $k$ is allowed. The set $\\{l<r\\}$ is covered by a uniformly bounded number of balls of radius $Cr$. Distances converge uniformly to a compact metric quotient $Z$ of the material manifold. Away from finitely many concentration points the metric convergence is smooth.*\n\n**Small-scale limits and genuine singularities.* At arbitrary sequences of centers and length scales $h_i\\to0$, with times tending to $T$, a subsequence of the rescaled slices converges to a complete noncollapsed Ricci-flat orbifold, smoothly away from finitely many genuine orbifold points. Each such point has a nontrivial finite group acting freely on $S^3$. A nonflat limit has a single Euclidean quotient end with nontrivial group. A flat limit is a global Euclidean quotient and has at most one exceptional point.*\n\n**Finite trees.* Within each concentrating cluster, maximal inequivalent nonflat limits form a finite rooted tree with a unique outermost entry. The joining regions have the quantitative charts of Lemma <a href=\"#geom:neck\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"geom:neck\">7</a> below.",
        "source_id": "PM-351.2"

          "anchor": "stmt-thm-degeneration",
          "exact_quote": "For a sufficiently small cap and all sufficiently late times, the following conclusions hold.*\n\n\n\n**Scale and energy bounds.* One has $$\\begin{aligned}\n cr^4\\leq\\mathop{\\mathrm{Vol}}_t B_t(x,r)&\\leq Cr^4,\n       &\\int_M|\\mathop{\\mathrm{Rm}}|^2\\,d\\mu_t&\\leq C,\\label{geom:volume-energy}\\\\\n |\\nabla^k\\mathop{\\mathrm{Rm}}|(x,t)&\\leq C_k l(x,t)^{-2-k},\n       &|\\nabla^k\\mathop{\\mathrm{Ric}}|(x,t)&\\leq C_k l(x,t)^{-1-k}.\n       \\label{eq:ricci-improvement}\n\\end{aligned}$$ Here $0<r\\leq l_0$ and each fixed $k$ is allowed. The set $\\{l<r\\}$ is covered by a uniformly bounded number of balls of radius $Cr$. Distances converge uniformly to a compact metric quotient $Z$ of the material manifold. Away from finitely many concentration points the metric convergence is smooth.*\n\n**Small-scale limits and genuine singularities.* At arbitrary sequences of centers and length scales $h_i\\to0$, with times tending to $T$, a subsequence of the rescaled slices converges to a complete noncollapsed Ricci-flat orbifold, smoothly away from finitely many genuine orbifold points. Each such point has a nontrivial finite group acting freely on $S^3$. A nonflat limit has a single Euclidean quotient end with nontrivial group. A flat limit is a global Euclidean quotient and has at most one exceptional point.*\n\n**Finite trees.* Within each concentrating cluster, maximal inequivalent nonflat limits form a finite rooted tree with a unique outermost entry. The joining regions have the quantitative charts of Lemma <a href=\"#geom:neck\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"geom:neck\">7</a> below.",

        }

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-geom-neck",
        "exact_quote": "Suppose annular regions have inner and outer radii $r_-\\ll r_+$ and every escaping intermediate annulus converges smoothly to a round flat quotient annulus. Assume for some $\\sigma>0$ and all needed finite orders that $$r^2|\\mathop{\\mathrm{Ric}}|_{k+2,r}\\leq\n C\\big((r/r_+)^\\sigma+(r_-/r)^\\sigma\\big).$$ Fix $0<\\mu<\\min(1,\\sigma)$. After removing fixed-factor boundary collars, for this fixed exponent, $$\\label{eq:neck-decay}\n r^2|\\mathop{\\mathrm{Rm}}|_{k,r}\\leq\n C\\big((r/r_+)^\\mu+(r_-/r)^\\mu\\big).$$ A single equivariant coordinate system on a shortened neck gives the same bound for $g-\\delta$, with scaled derivatives. Infinite half-necks are allowed. One may also use slowly varying coefficients on the boundary terms, when the forcing and boundary data obey their corresponding bounds.",
        "source_id": "PM-351.2"
      },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-root-scale",
        "exact_quote": "There is a fixed small $c_*>0$ such that the following definition detects the unique outermost entry. Choose a bounded continuous $\\psi:[0,1]\\to[0,\\infty)$, zero on $[0,c_*]$ and strictly positive on $[2c_*,1]$, and set $$\\label{geom:root-definition}\n a(t)=\\sup_{y\\in\\mathcal U}\n l(y,t)\\psi\\big(l(y,t)^2|\\mathop{\\mathrm{Rm}}|(y,t)\\big).$$ After shrinking $\\mathcal U$, this supremum is attained in its interior for every late time; it is positive and continuous, and $a(t)\\to0$. For every sequence of late times, scale $a(t)$ and any maximizing point represent the outermost nonflat entry. Every other nonflat entry has scale bounded by a constant times $a(t)$. The constants may be fixed after extracting the sequential tree.",
        "source_id": "PM-351.2"
      },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-geom-geodesic-radius",
        "exact_quote": "For any minimizing segment $\\gamma:[0,D]\\to M$ at a late time, parametrized by arclength, $$l(\\gamma(s),t)\\geq c\\min(l_0,s,D-s),\\qquad 0<s<D.$$ Consequently, whenever $d_t(x,y)\\leq Ch$ with $h$ small, its upper and lower time derivatives satisfy $$\\label{geom:distance-log}\n |\\partial_t d_t(x,y)|\\leq\n C'\\left(1+\\log^+(h/l(x,t))+\\log^+(h/l(y,t))\\right).$$ Time derivatives can be interpreted almost everywhere or by barriers.",
        "source_id": "PM-351.2"
      },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-geom-exterior-geometry",
        "exact_quote": "Fix a sufficiently high finite derivative order $k_{\\mathrm{geo}}$ and numbers $0<\\mu<\\sigma<1$. There are fixed constants $N_{\\mathrm{geom}},C,c_{\\mathrm{geom}}>0$ and outer radii $R_i^{\\mathrm{out}}\\to\\infty$ in interval units, with $\\epsilon_iR_i^{\\mathrm{out}}\\to0$, with the following property. At every time in the buffered interval the exterior region admits one equivariant coordinate system whose annuli satisfy $$\\label{geom:uniform-exterior}\n r^2|\\mathop{\\mathrm{Rm}}|_{k_{\\mathrm{geo}},r}+|g-\\delta|_{k_{\\mathrm{geo}},r}\n \\le C\\Theta_i(r),\\qquad\n \\Theta_i(r)=(a_i/r)^\\mu+\\delta_i r^\\mu,\\qquad\n \\delta_i=(R_i^{\\mathrm{out}})^{-\\mu}\\longrightarrow0$$ for $N_{\\mathrm{geom}}a_i\\le r\\le c_{\\mathrm{geom}}R_i^{\\mathrm{out}}$. The charts cover whole shortened distance bands with boundary margins; their nontrivial quotient group is fixed after subsequence extraction. Moreover $\\epsilon_i=o(\\delta_i)$. The constant $C$ is uniform in time and radius and is unchanged when the lower bound is restricted to $Na_i$ for any larger fixed $N$.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-para-local-smoothing",
        "exact_quote": "Consider a linear system on a regular parabolic patch, normalized to spatial radius and temporal duration of order one, with scalar uniformly elliptic principal part. Suppose its spatial coefficient derivatives through a sufficiently high fixed order are bounded. For nested cylinders $Q'\\Subset Q$ whose evaluation times have a positive past margin, a solution $U$ with source $F$ satisfies, for $1<p\\le2$, $$\\sup_{Q'}\\sum_{k=0}^j|D^kU|\n \\le C\\left[\n \\left(\\frac1{|J|}\\int_J\\|U(\\cdot,\\tau)\\|_{L^2(\\Omega)}^p\\,d\\tau\\right)^{1/p}\n +\\left(\\frac1{|J|}\\int_J\\|F(\\cdot,\\tau)\\|_{H^{j+3}(\\Omega)}^p\\,d\\tau\\right)^{1/p}\n \\right],\n \\label{para:local-mixed}$$ after enlarging $\\Omega\\times J$ inside $Q$ if necessary. The first input can instead be the normalized spacetime $L^p$ norm of $U$. Constants depend on the coefficient bounds and margins. Corresponding time $L^p$ estimates over longer windows follow by integrating sliding cylinders of unit duration.",
        "source_id": "PM-351.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-para-local-smoothing",
          "exact_quote": "Consider a linear system on a regular parabolic patch, normalized to spatial radius and temporal duration of order one, with scalar uniformly elliptic principal part. The first input can instead be the normalized spacetime $L^p$ norm of $U$. Constants depend on the coefficient bounds and margins. Corresponding time $L^p$ estimates over longer windows follow by integrating sliding cylinders of unit duration.",
          "source_id": "PM-351.2"

        "anchor": "stmt-para-radial-lemma",
        "exact_quote": "Suppose $PZ=Z$, $Z$ is compactly supported in time, and $E=(\\partial_\\tau-\\Delta_\\delta)Z$. For $1<p\\le2$, set $$z(s)=\\left\\|\\,\\|Z(s,\\cdot,\\tau)\\|_{L^2(S^3)}\\,\\right\\|_{L^p_\\tau}.$$ With either ordinary or constant-normalized time measure, $$\\mathcal Lz:=-z''-\\frac3s z'+\\frac3{s^2}z\n \\le\\left\\|\\,\\|E(s,\\cdot,\\tau)\\|_{L^2(S^3)}\\,\\right\\|_{L^p_\\tau}.\n \\label{para:radial-inequality}$$ For $p=2$ the same conclusion holds for a Hilbert direct sum of fields in different charts or time windows with the same radial variable.         "exact_quote": "Let $T$ be a spatially constant multiple of $\\mathop{\\mathrm{Ric}}$ on an annulus whose metric has scaled $C^{j+1}$ distance at most $e$ from $\\delta$.         "exact_quote": "There are fixed $N_0\\gg1$, $r_0>0$, and $0<\\alpha<\\min(\\mu,1)$, and a choice of $N_1$ in Equation <a href=\"#eq:root-energy\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:root-energy\">[eq:root-energy]</a>, such that $$\\begin{aligned}\n V(r)&\\le C\\left[(a/r)^{2+\\alpha}K\n             +\\widetilde\\delta_i\\bigl((a/r)^\\alpha+r^\\alpha\\bigr)\\right],\n \\label{para:exterior-v}\\\\\n A(r)&\\le C\\left[(a/r)^{2+\\alpha}K+\\widetilde\\delta_i\\right]\n \\label{para:exterior-a}\n\\end{aligned}$$ for $N_0a\\le r\\le r_0$, where $\\widetilde\\delta_i\\to0$ and $\\widetilde\\delta_i\\ge\\epsilon$.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-interior-ricci",
        "exact_quote": "Take any sequence of times with $\\eta(t)\\ne0$ and extract a tree as in Theorem <a href=\"#thm:degeneration\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"thm:degeneration\">6</a>. On its true compact and half-neck pieces in $B_t(x_t,D_0a)$, let $r$ be the physical length of the local regular patch, in $I$-units. For every fixed required derivative order $j$ there are $C,\\nu>0$ such that $$(r/a)^2|G|_{j,r}(t)\n       \\le C\\bigl(H_*(t)+\\epsilon\\bigr)e^{-\\nu D}.\n \\label{eq:interior-weighted}$$ Here $D=0$ on compact pieces, including truncated outer compact regions. On a joining neck $D$, up to bounded shifts, is the minimum of the logarithmic distances from the two ends. It is the half-neck coordinate used in Section <a href=\"#sec:static-input\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"sec:static-input\">2</a>. Constants may depend on the extracted tree, and the exponent may be decreased.",
        "source_id": "PM-351.2"
      },

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-thm-collar-extension",
        "exact_quote": "There are $\\sigma_*>0$, $R_*<\\infty$, and $C<\\infty$, depending only on $q,k$, the fixed shell choices, and the weight comparison constants, with the following property. If $\\sigma\\le\\sigma_*$ and $R\\ge R_*$, there is a metric $\\bar h$ on $\\mathcal E$ such that $$\\begin{split}\n \\|\\bar h-\\delta\\|_{H^k_q}\n   &\\le C\\|g-\\delta\\|_{H^k(C_1)},\\\\\n \\|g-\\bar h\\|_{H^k(C_1)}\n    +\\|\\mathop{\\mathrm{Ric}}(\\bar h)\\|_{H^{k-2}_{q+2}}\n   &\\le C m_1.\n\\end{split}\n\\label{eq:collar-extension}$$ The map $g|_{C_1}\\mapsto\\bar h-\\delta$ is real analytic on a fixed neighborhood of zero in collar $H^k$, and its first derivative into $H^k_q$ has uniformly bounded operator norm. It is independent of $R$, the weights, and the choice of an exterior realizing the collar data. There is also such an analytic extension $\\hat h$, equal to $g$ near the inner boundary, for which $$\\|\\hat h-\\delta\\|_{H^k_q}\\le C\\|g-\\delta\\|_{H^k(C_1)},\n \\qquad\n \\|\\mathop{\\mathrm{Ric}}(\\hat h)\\|_{H^{k-2}_{q+2}}\\le Cm_1,$$ and whose first derivative has the same bound.",
        "source_id": "PM-351.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-thm-collar-extension",
          "exact_quote": "There are $\\sigma_*>0$, $R_*<\\infty$, and $C<\\infty$, depending only on $q,k$, the fixed shell choices, and the weight comparison constants, with the following property. If $\\sigma\\le\\sigma_*$ and $R\\ge R_*$, there is a metric $\\bar h$ on $\\mathcal E$ such that $$\\begin{split}\n \\|\\bar h-\\delta\\|_{H^k_q}\n   &\\le C\\|g-\\delta\\|_{H^k(C_1)},\\\\\n \\|g-\\bar h\\|_{H^k(C_1)}\n    +\\|\\mathop{\\mathrm{Ric}}(\\bar h)\\|_{H^{k-2}_{q+2}}\n   &\\le C m_1.\n\\end{split}\n\\label{eq:collar-extension}$$ The map $g|_{C_1}\\mapsto\\bar h-\\delta$ is real analytic on a fixed neighborhood of zero in collar $H^k$, and its first derivative into $H^k_q$ has uniformly bounded operator norm. It is independent of $R$, the weights, and the choice of an exterior realizing the collar data. There is also such an analytic extension $\\hat h$, equal to $g$ near the inner boundary, for which $$\\|\\hat h-\\delta\\|_{H^k_q}\\le C\\|g-\\delta\\|_{H^k(C_1)},\n \\qquad\n \\|\\mathop{\\mathrm{Ric}}(\\hat h)\\|_{H^{k-2}_{q+2}}\\le Cm_1,$$ and whose first derivative has the same bound.",
          "source_id": "PM-351.2"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "There are $\\sigma_*>0$, $R_*<\\infty$, and $C<\\infty$, depending only on $q,k$, the fixed shell choices, and the weight comparison constants, with the following property.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-collar-potential",
        "exact_quote": "On a fixed smooth Euclidean annulus, ball, or fixed-width annular overlap in dimension four, there is a bounded linear extraction $v\\mapsto P v\\in H^{k+1}$ with $$\\|v-\\mathcal L_{Pv}\\delta\\|_{H^k}\n       \\le C\\|\\mathcal K(v)\\|_{H^{k-2}},\n \\qquad \\|Pv\\|_{H^{k+1}}\\le C\\|v\\|_{H^k}.\n \\label{collar:local-potential}$$ For vector fields on these domains, $$\\inf_{W\\in\\mathfrak e(4)}\\|V-W\\|_{H^{k+1}}\n           \\le C\\|\\mathcal L_V\\delta\\|_{H^k},\n \\label{collar:korn}$$ where $\\mathfrak e(4)=\\{x\\mapsto Ax+b:A^t=-A\\}$. All choices can be linear, normalized modulo this rigid-motion space, and equivariant. After dilation, the right side of the first inequality in Equation <a href=\"#collar:local-potential\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"collar:local-potential\">[collar:local-potential]</a> is multiplied by $r^2$, and vector norms in both inequalities are divided by $r$.",
        "source_id": "PM-351.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-collar-potential",
          "exact_quote": "On a fixed smooth Euclidean annulus, ball, or fixed-width annular overlap in dimension four, there is a bounded linear extraction $v\\mapsto P v\\in H^{k+1}$ with $$\\|v-\\mathcal L_{Pv}\\delta\\|_{H^k}\n       \\le C\\|\\mathcal K(v)\\|_{H^{k-2}},\n \\qquad \\|Pv\\|_{H^{k+1}}\\le C\\|v\\|_{H^k}.\n \\label{collar:local-potential}$$ For vector fields on these domains, $$\\inf_{W\\in\\mathfrak e(4)}\\|V-W\\|_{H^{k+1}}\n           \\le C\\|\\mathcal L_V\\delta\\|_{H^k},\n \\label{collar:korn}$$ where $\\mathfrak e(4)=\\{x\\mapsto Ax+b:A^t=-A\\}$. All choices can be linear, normalized modulo this rigid-motion space, and equivariant.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-collar-newton",
        "exact_quote": "Let $G(x)=c_4|x|^{-2}$ be the fundamental solution for the chosen sign of the Euclidean Laplacian. For $0<q<2$, its Newton operator is bounded $$\\Delta^{-1}:H^{k-2}_{q+2}(\\mathbb{R}^4)\\longrightarrow H^k_q(\\mathbb{R}^4).$$ For $1<q<2$, if $h\\in H^{k-3}_{q+3}(\\mathbb{R}^4)$ and $\\int_{\\mathbb{R}^4}h=0$ componentwise, then $$\\|\\Delta^{-1}h\\|_{H^{k-1}_{q+1}}\n             \\le C\\|h\\|_{H^{k-3}_{q+3}}.\n \\label{collar:zero-mass-newton}$$ Finally, on an annulus or ball of outer radius comparable to $R$, if $\\Delta W=H$, boundary strips control $W$ in scaled $H^{k-2}$ by $b_0$, and $c_r=r^2\\|H\\|_{H^{k-4}(r)}$, then $$\\sup_r\\|W\\|_{H^{k-2}(r)}\\le C\\left(b_0+\\sum_rc_r\\right).\n \\label{collar:green-sum}$$ The constant is independent of the number of shells. A filled ball has no inner boundary term; its innermost shell is replaced by a fixed ball.",

      },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-final-collar-position",
        "exact_quote": "Choose sufficiently small $\\sigma_0>0$ and sufficiently large fixed $N$. At a time near $\\mathop{\\mathrm{supp}}\\eta$, choose an exterior annular chart at scale $b$ with domain, in collar units, $\\{1/8<|x|<16\\}/\\Gamma$, retaining slightly larger boundary margins when necessary. Keep its map into the material manifold and its group fixed. For all sufficiently late $i$, throughout any interval on which its metric stays within $\\sigma_0$ of the flat metric through the required fixed derivative order, the following assertions hold. Every closed shortened collar lies at distance comparable to $b$ from the current center $x_t$; its middle bands cover the corresponding shortened distance annuli. The component on the designated inner side of a middle sphere contains $B_t(x_t,N_1a)$ and is contained in $B_t(x_t,Cb)$, with fixed constants.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-final-collar-bound",
        "exact_quote": "The residual in Equation <a href=\"#eq:collar-residual\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:collar-residual\">[eq:collar-residual]</a> for every active patch is bounded by a common nonnegative function $m(t)$ such that $$\\label{eq:collar-time-bound}\n \\sup_{\\mathop{\\mathrm{supp}}\\eta}m=o_i(1),\\qquad\n b^{-2}\\|\\eta m\\|_{L^2(I)}+\\|\\eta B_1\\|_{L^2(I)}\n \\le C\\bigl[(a/b)^{2+\\alpha}K+\\rho_i\\bigr],\n \\quad \\rho_i\\longrightarrow0.$$ The leading constant is independent of large $N$; $\\rho_i$ may depend on fixed $N$.",

<a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **Ricci flow** ([source](#sec:introduction)): A Ricci flow is a family of Riemannian metrics satisfying $\partial_tg=-2\mathop{\mathrm{Ric}}(g)$. Its scalar curvature $R=\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}_g\mathop{\mathrm{Ric}}$ controls the infinitesimal change of volume, whereas its full curvature tensor controls smooth continuation.
- **Spatial curvature radius** ([source](#stmt-geom-radius)): Fix a small cap $l_0>0$. At a material point $x\in M$ set $$l(x,t)=\sup\{0<r\leq l_0: \sup_{B_t(x,r)}\|\mathop{\mathrm{Rm}}\|\leq r^{-2}\}.$$ The definition uses the whole spatial ball, not just curvature at its center. The function $l$ is positive and continuous before $T$, and $\|l(x,t)-l(y,t)\|\leq d_t(x,y)$. Indeed a ball of radius $r-d_t(x,y)$ at $y$ is contained in the radius-$r$ ball at $x$, and its allowed curvature bound is larger. Continuity in time follows by compactness and smooth dependence of the metric, using radii slightly smaller or larger than the supremum.
- **Renormalized Einstein–Hilbert functional** ([source](#eq:energy-functional)): Define, in physical root units, $$E(g)=\lim_{D\to\infty}\left\{ \int_{r<D}R(g)\,d\mu_g- \int_{r=D}(\partial_jg_{ij}-\partial_ig_{jj})n_i\,dS_\delta \right\}. \label{eq:energy-functional}$$ All chart integrals are quotient integrals. The two terms inside the braces need not converge separately.

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **step-heat-localization** [claim](#stmt-thm-degeneration) / [proof](#geom:local-estimates): Entropy monotonicity and logarithmic Sobolev inequality give heat kernel bounds, then a bootstrap on the curvature radius with cutoffs yields derivative bounds and the Ricci improvement. -> Volume bounds and Ricci improvement
- **step-energy-bad-set** [claim](#stmt-thm-degeneration) / [proof](#geom:energy-comparison): Integral curvature estimate with Gauss–Bonnet and Gronwall gives slice energy; point picking bounds the volume of the bad set and the cubic Ricci integral. -> Energy bound and bad-set volume bound
- **step-neck-lemma** [claim](#stmt-geom-neck) / [proof](#proof-stmt-geom-neck): Contradiction by dilating at approximate maximizers, harmonic limit tensor and Bianchi identity; gauge extraction and chart gluing. -> Weighted curvature decay and neck coordinates
- **step-root-scale** [claim](#stmt-prop-root-scale) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-root-scale): Cutoff-weighted maximum of the curvature radius detects the outermost entry of the finite tree. -> Continuous outermost scale
- **step-short-motion** [claim](#stmt-geom-slow-motion) / [proof](#proof-stmt-geom-slow-motion): Logarithmic distance derivative bound integrated over short intervals, discarding a small bad set. -> Negligible motion of geometry on short intervals
- **step-buffered** [claim](#stmt-prop-buffered-intervals) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-buffered-intervals): Successive times of fixed logarithmic scale change, a chain argument for buffer sizes, and parabolic rescaling. -> Buffered rescaled intervals
- **step-exterior-geometry** [claim](#stmt-geom-exterior-geometry) / [proof](#proof-stmt-geom-exterior-geometry): Apply the neck estimate uniformly in time on the exterior annuli. -> Uniform exterior coordinates
- **step-exterior-ricci** [claim](#stmt-prop-exterior-ricci) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-exterior-ricci): Spherical projection with radial inequality, local smoothing, mean-derivative control and a closed recurrence over dyadic shells. -> Exterior decay of the cut-off Ricci tensor
- **step-interior-ricci** [claim](#stmt-prop-interior-ricci) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-interior-ricci): Maximal function bound, local smoothing and a radial barrier on deep necks. -> Single-time weighted Ricci estimate
- **step-collar-extension** [claim](#stmt-thm-collar-extension) / [proof](#thm:collar-extension): Filled-space generalized inverse, analytic inverse function theorem, comparison gauge and Newton estimates to bound the residual. -> Analytic exterior extension
- **step-variation** [claim](#stmt-final-variation) / [proof](#proof-stmt-final-variation): First variation of the functional on patches with partition of unity; core gives positive term, collar tail is bounded using the decay exponent above one. -> Lower bound for functional variation
- **step-functional-smallness** [claim](#stmt-final-functional-smallness) / [proof](#proof-stmt-final-functional-smallness): Extract a fixed tree along any bad sequence, verify the hypotheses of the static theorem, and contradict a failing ratio. -> Uniform functional smallness
- **step-energy-zero** [claim](#stmt-final-energy-vanishes) / [proof](#proof-stmt-final-energy-vanishes): Integrate by parts over the partition and absorb the loss by choosing the collar factor large. -> Root energy tends to zero
- **step-root-fixed** [claim](#stmt-final-root-fixed) / [proof](#proof-stmt-final-root-fixed): Good dense points preserve distances, giving a pointed isometry of the endpoint limits and equal root maxima. -> Root scale cannot change
- **step-extension** [claim](#stmt-thm-extension) / [proof](#pm-h-33): No concentration gives a curvature bound, smooth limit metric at the final time, and Ricci–DeTurck continuation. -> Smooth extension on the same manifold

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **inv-scalar-bound-extends-flow**: On a closed connected smooth four-manifold, a smooth Ricci flow on a finite interval whose scalar curvature stays uniformly bounded extends smoothly on the same manifold past the final time and agrees with the given flow before it.
- **inv-finite-time-forces-scalar-blowup**: If the maximal smooth existence time of a Ricci flow on a closed four-manifold is finite, the maximum of the scalar curvature is unbounded as the time approaches that final time.
- **inv-collar-extension-small-regime**: The collar extension produces an exterior metric with controlled weighted norms and Ricci residual only when the shell weight sum is small and the outer radius is large enough.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **anti-other-dimensions**: Use the extension statement as a criterion for continuing flows in dimensions other than four. -> The proof relies on four-dimensional energy, orbifold and bubble-tree structure, so nothing is implied elsewhere.
- **anti-noncompact-or-boundary**: Apply the continuation claim to manifolds with boundary or noncompact manifolds. -> Compactness without boundary is used for maximum principles and for the finiteness of the energy and covering arguments.
- **anti-non-riemannian-flows**: Transfer the result to evolution equations that are not Ricci flow of Riemannian metrics. -> The estimates use the Ricci flow equation, its evolution identities and the Riemannian structure.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `not_applicable`. The paper's results are existence and regularity statements proved by contradiction through compactness, subsequence extraction of bubble trees and analytic estimates; no finite procedure with explicit steps computes an object, so no runnable reference code or independent oracle can be given.

<a id="pm-s5"></a>
## 5. Domain engineering contracts

| domain | applies | strength | maturity | basis | why |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| software | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The theorem is an existence statement for a smooth continuation of a flow on a closed four-manifold. It prescribes no procedure that a program could execute, so it only supplies a mathematical specification. |
| games_simulation | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The result concerns smooth Riemannian metrics on a closed real four-manifold, not interactive or physical simulation. It gives no discretisation or stability guarantee for any simulator. |
| tools | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | No tool, checker or verifier is produced. The proof is analytic and compactness-based, and the paper only records a conclusion about smooth continuation. |
| algorithms | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The argument proceeds by contradiction, extracting subsequences and trees from a hypothetical concentration point. No step is a finite computation with stated cost, so no algorithm can be read off. |
| data_structures | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The finite rooted tree of limits is an analytic bookkeeping device obtained by subsequence extraction, not a maintained data structure with operations or costs. |
| hardware_accelerators | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The paper gives no arithmetic kernel, precision analysis or parallel schedule. The estimates are qualitative continuum bounds with unspecified constants. |
| crypto_security | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Nothing in the paper concerns adversaries, hardness assumptions or secrecy. A bound on scalar curvature has no security semantics. |
| machine_learning | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | No learning objective, dataset or generalisation statement appears. The renormalised functional is a geometric quantity on Ricci-flat trees, not a loss to be trained. |
| optimisation_operations_research | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The only extremal objects are maxima defining the root scale, used inside a proof. There is no feasible set, objective or solution method for an optimisation problem. |
| distributed_systems | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The paper has no notion of nodes, messages or consensus. Its patches in time are analytic partitions of unity, and the text states that no tree is chosen smoothly through time. |
| other | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The main value is mathematical: a four-dimensional statement that bounded scalar curvature prevents a finite-time singularity on a closed manifold. It does not transfer to other dimensions or to non-closed settings. |

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **L-closed-four-manifold** (hypothesis): The extension theorem is stated only for a closed connected smooth four-manifold with a smooth initial metric. Disconnected closed manifolds are covered componentwise, and nothing else is claimed. Blocked use: ['Applying the continuation claim to manifolds of dimension other than four', 'Applying the continuation claim to manifolds with boundary or to noncompact manifolds']
- **L-uniform-scalar-bound** (hypothesis): Smooth continuation is derived only when the scalar curvature is uniformly bounded over the whole interval up to the finite final time. A bound that holds only on part of the interval, or only along a subsequence of times, is not the stated hypothesis. Blocked use: ['Concluding continuation from a scalar curvature bound that is only local in time or only observed on a finite sample', 'Using the theorem when the scalar curvature is bounded on one side only and the absolute-value bound is not checked']
- **L-proof-by-contradiction-nonconstructive** (nonconstructive): The argument rules out a concentration point by contradiction, extracting subsequences and trees from a hypothetical singular sequence. It yields no explicit rate, procedure or computable bound for the extension time. Blocked use: ['Reading an algorithm for locating bubble trees from the proof', 'Using the proof to give a computable lower bound on the continuation time epsilon']
- **L-sequential-tree-extraction** (formal_scope): The bubble tree and its identifications are obtained only along an extracted sequence. The paper explicitly does not claim that one tree, or one family of identifications, varies smoothly in time. Blocked use: ['Treating the tree as a time-dependent object that can be differentiated or tracked continuously', 'Assuming a single tree works uniformly for all late times']
- **L-static-input-sequential** (formal_scope): The static tree inequality is imported from a companion article and holds as a sequential little-oh on a fixed limiting tree, with parameters fixed first. It gives no uniform quantitative constant across different trees. Blocked use: ['Using the inequality as a uniform quantitative bound across different trees', 'Treating the little-oh as holding without first fixing the tree and the parameters']
- **L-collar-extension-small-regime** (hypothesis): The collar extension and its Ricci residual bound are asserted only when the shell weight sum is below a threshold and the outer radius is above a threshold, both depending on fixed choices. Blocked use: ['Applying the collar extension to a collar whose weights are not small', 'Applying the collar extension with an outer radius below the required threshold']
- **L-ineffective-constants** (approximation): All constants in the estimates depend on a fixed finite differentiation order and, after extraction, on a fixed tree. They are not given numerically. Blocked use: ['Sizing a numerical tolerance or resolution from the constants in the estimates', 'Claiming a quantitative time-to-singularity from the stated bounds']

<a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


We prove that a smooth Ricci flow on a closed real four-manifold extends on the same manifold through every finite time at which its scalar curvature remains uniformly bounded. This resolves the scalar-curvature extension problem in dimension four.

<a id="sec:introduction"></a>
### Introduction

A Ricci flow is a family of Riemannian metrics satisfying $\partial_tg=-2\mathop{\mathrm{Ric}}(g)$. Its scalar curvature $R=\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}_g\mathop{\mathrm{Ric}}$ controls the infinitesimal change of volume, whereas its full curvature tensor controls smooth continuation. The scalar-curvature extension problem asks whether a uniform scalar bound prevents a finite-time singularity. We prove its positive resolution on closed manifolds in real dimension four.

<a id="stmt-thm-extension"></a>
<div id="thm:extension" class="theorem">

**Theorem 1** (Smooth extension). *Let $M$ be a closed connected smooth four-manifold, let $0<T<\infty$, and let $g(t)$, $0\le t<T$, be a smooth Ricci flow with smooth initial metric. If $$\sup_{M\times[0,T)}|R(g(t))|<\infty,$$ then there are $\varepsilon>0$ and a smooth Ricci flow $\widetilde g(t)$ on $M$, $0\le t<T+\varepsilon$, such that $\widetilde g(t)=g(t)$ for every $t<T$.*

</div>

The same conclusion holds without connectedness. The closed manifold $M$ has finitely many connected components, each a closed smooth four-manifold. The restricted flows satisfy the hypotheses of Theorem <a href="#thm:extension" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:extension">1</a> at the common time $T$, so each extends to $T+\varepsilon_j$ for some $\varepsilon_j>0$. Taking $\varepsilon=\min_j\varepsilon_j$ and combining these flows gives the required extension on $M$.

<a id="stmt-cor-scalar-blowup"></a>
<div id="cor:scalar-blowup" class="corollary">

**Corollary 2**. *If a Ricci flow on a closed smooth four-manifold has finite maximal smooth existence time $T$, then $$\limsup_{t\uparrow T}\max_{x\in M}R(g(t))(x)=+\infty.$$*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-cor-scalar-blowup"><summary>Proof of stmt-cor-scalar-blowup</summary>

*Proof.* The equation $(\partial_t-\Delta)R=2|\mathop{\mathrm{Ric}}|^2$ and the maximum principle give a scalar lower bound on a finite time interval. A finite upper limit in the assertion would therefore give a uniform absolute bound near $T$, and smoothness supplies the bound on the remaining compact time interval. The componentwise extension just described contradicts maximality. ◻

</details>

<a id="pm-h-3"></a>
#### The extension problem and its geometric obstruction

Hamilton introduced Ricci flow and established its short-time existence on closed manifolds (Hamilton 1982) <a id="pm-cite-1"></a>[[Hamilton1982](#ref-Hamilton1982)]. A compact flow continues while its full curvature remains bounded. Šešum showed that a uniform bound for the Ricci tensor also suffices (Šešum 2005) <a id="pm-cite-2"></a>[[Sesum2005](#ref-Sesum2005)]. A scalar bound is weaker: in a possible curvature blowup, the scalar curvature may vanish after rescaling while the full curvature stays nonzero. The limiting geometry can therefore be Ricci-flat without being flat.

Bamler and Zhang developed heat-kernel and curvature estimates under scalar bounds, including the four-dimensional energy and compactness theory (Bamler and Zhang 2017, 2019) <a id="pm-cite-3"></a>[[BamlerZhangI](#ref-BamlerZhangI), [BamlerZhangII](#ref-BamlerZhangII)]. Their work also gives smooth extension when $H_2(M;\mathbb F)=0$ for every field $\mathbb F$ (Bamler and Zhang 2017, arXiv version, Corollary 1.10) <a id="pm-cite-4"></a>[[BamlerZhangI](#ref-BamlerZhangI)]. Simon constructed continuation from the limiting space by an orbifold Ricci flow (Simon 2020a) <a id="pm-cite-5"></a>[[SimonExtension](#ref-SimonExtension)]. Bamler’s convergence theory gives smooth convergence away from a set of codimension at least four (Bamler 2018) <a id="pm-cite-6"></a>[[BamlerConvergence](#ref-BamlerConvergence)]; four-dimensional convergence under spatial scalar-integrability hypotheses is also studied by Liu and Simon (Liu and Simon 2026) <a id="pm-cite-7"></a>[[LiuSimon](#ref-LiuSimon)]. These compactness conclusions permit concentrated curvature and orbifold points. The assertion here is smooth continuation of the given flow on its original manifold. The scalar extension problem and its known special cases are discussed in (Li and Li 2026, sec. 2) <a id="pm-cite-8"></a>[[LiLiSurvey](#ref-LiLiSurvey)]. Finite-time scalar blowup was established for closed Kähler–Ricci flows by Zhang (Zhang 2010) <a id="pm-cite-9"></a>[[Zhang2010Kahler](#ref-Zhang2010Kahler)] and for compact Ricci flows with a global Type I bound, $\sup_M|\mathop{\mathrm{Rm}}(g(t))|\le C/(T-t)$, by Enders, Müller and Topping (Enders, Müller, and Topping 2011) <a id="pm-cite-10"></a>[[EndersMuellerTopping2011](#ref-EndersMuellerTopping2011)]. More recently, Buzano and Di Matteo proved scalar blowup at local Type I singular points without a global Type I assumption (Buzano and Di Matteo 2026, Definition 1.4 and Theorem 1.1) <a id="pm-cite-11"></a>[[BuzanoDiMatteo2026](#ref-BuzanoDiMatteo2026)]. Here “local Type I” means $0<\limsup_{t\uparrow T}(T-t)r_{\mathop{\mathrm{Rm}}}(p,t)^{-2}<\infty$, where $r_{\mathop{\mathrm{Rm}}}$ is their parabolic curvature scale at the point $p$. That result addresses a specified blowup regime; the argument below allows unrestricted rates at the hypothetical concentration points.

The spatial degeneration analysis has its origins in the theory of Einstein metrics. Anderson’s compactness theorem produces orbifold limits with smooth convergence on their regular parts (Anderson 1989, Theorem C) <a id="pm-cite-12"></a>[[Anderson1989](#ref-Anderson1989)]. Bando’s bubbling analysis, including its correction, organizes Ricci-flat bubbles and quantitative necks (Bando 1990a, 1990b) <a id="pm-cite-13"></a>[[Bando1990](#ref-Bando1990), [BandoCorrection1990](#ref-BandoCorrection1990)]; the work of Bando, Kasue and Nakajima develops coordinates at infinity under curvature decay and volume-growth hypotheses (Bando, Kasue, and Nakajima 1989) <a id="pm-cite-14"></a>[[BandoKasueNakajima1989](#ref-BandoKasueNakajima1989)]. Cheeger and Tian’s curvature-energy estimates provide a further important part of this Einstein four-manifold theory (Cheeger and Tian 2006) <a id="pm-cite-15"></a>[[CheegerTian2006](#ref-CheegerTian2006)]. Our slices need not be Einstein. We prove the required geometric statements below using the scalar-bound estimates and retaining the Ricci forcing in the neck argument.

At a hypothetical concentration point, successive spatial rescalings produce a finite tree of Ricci-flat pieces. A vertex represents a nonflat limit; its singular points may contain smaller vertices. The outermost nonflat vertex has a distinguished length scale $a(t)$. The central problem is to rule out contraction of this outermost scale while allowing arbitrarily smaller descendants.

<a id="pm-h-4"></a>
#### The static functional and the flow estimates

Retain the true metric from the cluster through an annular collar of radius $b=Na$, with $N$ large and fixed, and attach an asymptotically locally Euclidean end. On that end write the metric in Euclidean quotient coordinates. The renormalized Einstein–Hilbert functional is $$E(g)=\lim_{D\to\infty}\left[
 \int_{\{r<D\}}R(g)\,d\mu_g-
 \int_{\{r=D\}}(\partial_jg_{ij}-\partial_ig_{jj})n_i\,dS_\delta
 \right].$$ The boundary subtraction cancels the scalar curvature’s linear divergence term. The functional scales by the square of the length unit, and its first variation pairs the metric velocity with $\tfrac12 Rg-\mathop{\mathrm{Ric}}$.

The companion article (OpenAI 2026) <a id="pm-cite-16"></a>[[StaticTrees](#ref-StaticTrees)] proves a sequential inequality $E=o(M)$ on each fixed limiting tree, where $M$ is its weighted, dimensionless Ricci error. Section <a href="#sec:static-input" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:static-input">2</a> records the complete input, including the end weights, derivative orders and matching quotient groups. Our task is both to verify those hypotheses for metrics obtained from the flow and to compare their functional variation with actual spacetime Ricci energy.

<figure id="fig:scales">

<figcaption>The outermost scale $a$, its smaller descendants, and the material collar at radius $b = Na$. An edge of logarithmic length $L$ changes the length unit by $e^{−2L}$. The collar extension preserves the inner geometry and supplies an ALE end. Incidence and scale are schematic.</figcaption>
</figure>

<a id="pm-figsrc-fig-scales"></a>

- A vertical bracket to the right of the outermost piece is labeled "fixed collar" and "$b = Na$".

Two flow estimates are decisive. The divergence constraint on Ricci removes the critical radial harmonic mode on a nearly flat annulus. This improves annular decay enough that the loss outside the collar is small compared with the energy inside the root. The exterior is then constructed as an analytic function of the metric on one fixed material collar. Longer exterior coordinates enter only an error estimate. Consequently the extension can be differentiated on time patches without choosing a smoothly varying bubble tree. The collar construction uses the classical compatibility between metric strains and linearized curvature, together with Korn control modulo rigid motions (Khavkine 2017; Ciarlet and Ciarlet 2005) <a id="pm-cite-17"></a>[[Khavkine2017](#ref-Khavkine2017), [CiarletCiarlet2005](#ref-CiarletCiarlet2005)]. The scale-specific estimates, filled-space inverse and dependence on the fixed collar are proved in Section <a href="#sec:collar" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:collar">5</a>.

<a id="pm-h-5"></a>
#### Proof structure

Section <a href="#sec:geometry" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:geometry">3</a> establishes the scale estimates, genuine orbifold limits and finite trees needed here. It defines a continuous outermost scale and selects buffered time intervals on which that scale changes by a fixed factor. After parabolic normalization, the interval has fixed duration and its root length $a$ tends to zero.

Section <a href="#sec:parabolic" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:parabolic">4</a> controls Ricci on exterior annuli and on the root’s compact and joining regions. A time maximal function converts root spacetime energy into the single-time weighted error required by the static theorem. Section <a href="#sec:collar" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:collar">5</a> constructs the collar-dependent exterior and proves an error bound independent of its length.

Section <a href="#sec:contradiction" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:contradiction">6</a> applies the static theorem separately to arbitrary sequences of active time patches. If $K^2$ denotes the root spacetime Ricci energy divided by $a^4$, the positive functional variation and its static smallness imply $$cK^2\le o(1)(K+1)^2.$$ Hence $K\to0$, without assuming it was bounded. Dense sets of material points then preserve distances at scale $a$ between the two endpoints. The resulting pointed isometry preserves the curvature-radius maximum defining the outermost scale. This contradicts its prescribed factor change. The absence of concentration gives a smooth terminal metric and continuation on $M$.

<a id="pm-h-6"></a>
#### Conventions

We use $\Delta=\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}\nabla^2$. A tensor norm at scale $r$ includes $r^j$ on its $j$th spatial derivative, while leaving its pointwise magnitude unchanged. Tensor magnitudes use the current metric unless specified otherwise. Quotient-chart components are Cartesian components on the Euclidean cover; all constructions are equivariant and descend to the quotient.

Constants may depend on a fixed finite differentiation order. After extraction they may also depend on a fixed finite tree. Uniformity in a sequence index, a length, or the collar factor $N$ is stated where needed. A sequential little-oh is taken after fixing the parameters in its statement.

<a id="sec:static-input"></a>
### The static input and its normalization

We record the precise input from the companion article (OpenAI 2026, Theorem 2.2) <a id="pm-cite-18"></a>[[StaticTrees](#ref-StaticTrees)]. Its constants and small exponents belong to one fixed limiting tree. No uniform exponent across different trees is needed. The elementary functional identities are included here to fix the normalizations used in the flow calculation. The remaining static proof, including path selection and the augmented elliptic construction, is supplied by the complete companion.

<a id="ell:setup"></a>
#### The geometric data and the functional

<a id="ell:end-decay"></a>
Let $\mathcal T$ be a finite rooted tree of four-dimensional Ricci-flat spaces $(V_v,h_v)$. A vertex has one exterior end and one punctured end for each child. Every end has Cartesian coordinates on a Euclidean annulus modulo a finite subgroup of $O(4)$ acting freely on $S^3$. The group on a joined end is nontrivial. The two groups on an edge are identified; a fixed orthogonal identification is included in the charts. Put $\tau=\log r$ on an exterior end and $\tau=-\log r$ at a puncture. In these charts, in vertex units, $$h_v=\delta+O(e^{-\mu\tau})
 \label{ell:end-decay}$$ on joined ends, with derivatives at scale, for some $\mu>0$. On the unjoined exterior end of the root we require instead $h_v-\delta=O(r^{-q})$, where $q>1$. All remaining parts are smooth compact manifolds with the indicated boundary collars. Bounds through order $20$ suffice in the data below.

For an edge $e$ from $p$ to $c$, truncate both end coordinates at $\tau=L_e$ and identify the boundary collars by <a id="ell:scales"></a>$$x_p=e^{-2L_e}x_c,\qquad a_c=a_p e^{-2L_e},\qquad a_{\rm root}=1.
 \label{ell:scales}$$ Thus the metric tensor in vertex units is multiplied by $a_v^2$ in physical units. The physical radius $\rho=a_vr$ agrees in both charts, and $\tau_p+\tau_c=2L_e$. Gluing just the boundary faces, with their compatible smooth collars, gives the same manifold.

<a id="eq:tree-ricci-error"></a>
Consider a sequence of metrics $g$ for which every $L_e\to\infty$, which converges to $h_v$ on each compact subset of every open vertex. Assume the same end estimates as in Equation <a href="#ell:end-decay" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="ell:end-decay">[ell:end-decay]</a> on each joining half, and the stated root estimate, with uniform finite derivative bounds. On a compact core put $\rho=a_v$. Suppose $M\to0$ and $$|\rho^2\mathop{\mathrm{Ric}}(g)|_j\le CM
 \begin{cases}
 e^{-\mu\tau},&\text{on joined half-ends},\\
 r^{-q},&\text{on the root exterior end},\\
 1,&\text{on vertex cores},
 \end{cases}
 \qquad j=10.
 \label{eq:tree-ricci-error}$$ Here the tensor magnitude is physical, and the subscript includes covariant derivatives multiplied by the corresponding powers of $\rho$. The metric bounds convert these to coordinate estimates. Decreasing $\mu$ or $q$, while retaining $\mu>0$ and $q>1$, is allowed.

<a id="eq:energy-functional"></a>
Define, in physical root units, $$E(g)=\lim_{D\to\infty}\left\{
 \int_{r<D}R(g)\,d\mu_g-
 \int_{r=D}(\partial_jg_{ij}-\partial_ig_{jj})n_i\,dS_\delta
 \right\}.
 \label{eq:energy-functional}$$ All chart integrals are quotient integrals. The two terms inside the braces need not converge separately.

The boundary subtraction is the mass term in the renormalized Einstein–Hilbert expression discussed by Deruelle and Ozuch (Deruelle and Ozuch 2025, Definition 4.2, Remark 4.3, and Proposition 4.4) <a id="pm-cite-19"></a>[[DeruelleOzuch2025](#ref-DeruelleOzuch2025)]. Their ALE version of Perelman’s functional also minimizes over an auxiliary potential; the functional $E$ here is the unminimized scalar-curvature-minus-flux expression.

<a id="stmt-ell-functional"></a>
<div id="ell:functional" class="lemma">

<a id="ell:first-variation"></a>
**Lemma 3** (Renormalization and first variation). *The functional in Equation <a href="#eq:energy-functional" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:energy-functional">[eq:energy-functional]</a> is defined on the preceding metrics. For a variation with the same root decay, $$DE_g(\dot g)=\int\left\langle\tfrac12R(g)g-\mathop{\mathrm{Ric}}(g),\dot g\right\rangle_g
 \,d\mu_g.
 \label{ell:first-variation}$$ It is invariant under diffeomorphisms isotopic to the identity whose root displacement, including scaled derivatives throughout the isotopy, is $O(r^{1-q'})$ for some $q'>1$. A global length scaling by $s$, accompanied by a coordinate dilation at infinity restoring the leading tensor $\delta$, multiplies $E$ by $s^2$. Under Equation <a href="#eq:tree-ricci-error" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:tree-ricci-error">[eq:tree-ricci-error]</a>, $|E(g)|\le CM$.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-ell-functional"><summary>Proof of stmt-ell-functional</summary>

*Proof.* Write $g=\delta+p$ on the root end. The scalar-curvature density is $$R(g)\sqrt{\det g}=
 \partial_i(\partial_jp_{ij}-\partial_ip_{jj})+
 O(|p||\partial^2p|+|\partial p|^2).$$ The last expression is $O(r^{-2q-2})$ and its radial integral is bounded by $C\int_{r_0}^\infty r^{1-2q}\,dr$. This proves convergence. The linear boundary term in the variation of scalar curvature cancels the variation of the subtracted integral. Every remaining boundary product is $O(D^{2-2q})$; it tends to zero. Integration by parts therefore gives Equation <a href="#ell:first-variation" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="ell:first-variation">[ell:first-variation]</a>. For a Lie derivative, the contracted Bianchi identity makes the interior divergence vanish. The remaining boundary term vanishes with the stated displacement decay. Integration along the isotopy gives invariance. Changing variables under a dilation in Equation <a href="#eq:energy-functional" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:energy-functional">[eq:energy-functional]</a> gives the scaling law.

Differentiate this law at $s=1$. Choose the compensating dilation to be the identity inside a fixed outer root region. Its metric variation equals $2g$ on the interior and decays like $r^{-q}$ on the root tail. Its pairing with Ricci on the root is integrable by $q>1$. On a punctured half the interior estimate costs $$CM a_p^2\int_{\tau_0}^{L_e}e^{-(2+\mu)\tau}\,d\tau\le CM a_p^2.$$ On a child exterior half the corresponding quantity is $$CM a_c^2\int_{\tau_0}^{L_e}e^{(2-\mu)\tau}\,d\tau
 \le CM a_p^2(1+L_e)e^{-\min(2+\mu,4)L_e},$$ with a harmless change of constant for the fixed lower endpoint. The finite sum of core contributions is bounded as well, since $a_v\le1$. Equation <a href="#ell:first-variation" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="ell:first-variation">[ell:first-variation]</a> now gives $2|E(g)|\le CM$. In particular $E=0$ when $\mathop{\mathrm{Ric}}=0$. ◻

</details>

<a id="stmt-thm-tree-inequality"></a>
<div id="thm:tree-inequality" class="theorem">

**Theorem 4** (Static tree inequality (OpenAI 2026, Theorem 2.2) <a id="pm-cite-20"></a>[[StaticTrees](#ref-StaticTrees)]). *For the fixed tree and the sequences just specified, <a id="ell:little-oh"></a>$$E(g)=o(M).
 \label{ell:little-oh}$$ For $M=0$ the assertion means $E(g)=0$.*

</div>

The companion proof constructs a finite-dimensional analytic family with complex length parameters. It does not assume that Einstein deformations are unobstructed, or that the given metrics or the coefficients of a fixed core metric are spatially analytic.

The theorem is applied in Proposition <a href="#final:functional-smallness" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="final:functional-smallness">28</a>. There we verify every hypothesis after extracting a fixed tree from an arbitrary sequence of active times and material collars. The root exterior has decay exponent greater than one because it is replaced by the extension of Section <a href="#sec:collar" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:collar">5</a>; the true joining necks retain their own positive decay exponent.

<a id="sec:geometry"></a>
### Small-scale geometry and the outermost scale

Throughout this section, $(M^4,g(t))$, $0\leq t<T$, is the closed Ricci flow under consideration and $|R|\leq C_R$. Constants may depend on this flow, $T$, and a specified finite differentiation order. They are independent of the small scales subsequently chosen. For a tensor $A$, write $|A|_{k,r}=\sum_{j=0}^k r^j|\nabla^j A|$; on coordinate annuli the analogous notation uses Cartesian derivatives. We first establish the geometric input needed in the later analytic argument. The bounded-scalar-curvature estimates in (Bamler and Zhang 2017, 2019) <a id="pm-cite-21"></a>[[BamlerZhangI](#ref-BamlerZhangI), [BamlerZhangII](#ref-BamlerZhangII)] give background for these estimates; the scale selection, tree organization, and motion assertions needed here are included below.

<a id="stmt-geom-radius"></a>
<div id="geom:radius" class="definition">

**Definition 5** (Spatial curvature radius). Fix a small cap $l_0>0$. At a material point $x\in M$ set $$l(x,t)=\sup\{0<r\leq l_0:
       \sup_{B_t(x,r)}|\mathop{\mathrm{Rm}}|\leq r^{-2}\}.$$ The definition uses the whole spatial ball, not just curvature at its center. The function $l$ is positive and continuous before $T$, and $|l(x,t)-l(y,t)|\leq d_t(x,y)$. Indeed a ball of radius $r-d_t(x,y)$ at $y$ is contained in the radius-$r$ ball at $x$, and its allowed curvature bound is larger. Continuity in time follows by compactness and smooth dependence of the metric, using radii slightly smaller or larger than the supremum.

</div>

<a id="stmt-thm-degeneration"></a>
<div id="thm:degeneration" class="theorem">

**Theorem 6** (Scale estimates and degeneration). *For a sufficiently small cap and all sufficiently late times, the following conclusions hold.*

<a id="geom:volume-energy"></a>
<a id="eq:ricci-improvement"></a>
**Scale and energy bounds.* One has $$\begin{aligned}
 cr^4\leq\mathop{\mathrm{Vol}}_t B_t(x,r)&\leq Cr^4,
       &\int_M|\mathop{\mathrm{Rm}}|^2\,d\mu_t&\leq C,\label{geom:volume-energy}\\
 |\nabla^k\mathop{\mathrm{Rm}}|(x,t)&\leq C_k l(x,t)^{-2-k},
       &|\nabla^k\mathop{\mathrm{Ric}}|(x,t)&\leq C_k l(x,t)^{-1-k}.
       \label{eq:ricci-improvement}
\end{aligned}$$ Here $0<r\leq l_0$ and each fixed $k$ is allowed. The set $\{l<r\}$ is covered by a uniformly bounded number of balls of radius $Cr$. Distances converge uniformly to a compact metric quotient $Z$ of the material manifold. Away from finitely many concentration points the metric convergence is smooth.*
